## Grand Prix Madrid 2010

![](https://i.ormos.uk/4e/97/4e9772bef5753d948a9232f099cd0dfd3b98367aa2c5da2a15de63adc1fd6e5d.jpg)

Grand Prix–Madrid, the largest **Magic** tournament of all time, is at an end. 2,227 players were there at the start, but at the very end, it came down to just two. David Do Anh of the Czech Republic had bested Tomoharu Saito, and Andreas Müller had made it through an incredible battle with Richard Bland, to set up for a final between Reanimator and Ad Nauseam Tendrils. These finals came down to the turn of a card, as an agonising Ad Nauseam turned on its master ending the deciding game in decisive fashion. With this, Andreas Müller of Germany found himself on top of the world here in Madrid.

All weekend we have seen innovation and invention, triumphs and bitter defeats as the great and the good from around the world have converged on Madrid for a colossal 20 rounds of Legacy competition. We have seen new takes on old decks, and old cards in new ones, only adding to the diverse metagame for which Legacy is so justly popular. With a whole world of magical cards available to build with, Legacy has shown itself to be full of cool interactions, making for an amazingly entertaining format.

As the lights fade here in Madrid, and we look to Kuala Lumpur, Malaysia for the next Grand Prix in the calendar in a few short weeks, there are over 2000 people leaving the site with a story to tell. For one player in particular, this story had a very happy ending. Congratulations to Andreas Müller, the Grand Prix–Madrid 2010 Champion!

## Quarterfinals - Sven Dijt vs. Andreas Müller

*by Tobias Henke*

![](https://i.ormos.uk/a7/31/a731f2605bfb79d6bd919d96ab938afe51909b4d9f5c1a3f7da1405ffced9639.jpg)*Sven Dijt*

This is the first Grand Prix top eight for Andreas Müller (not to be confused with fellow German player Andre Müller who got a couple). Facing him is Sven Dijt of the Netherlands who got his first Grand Prix top eight a wee little while ago. In fact, he made top eight at the first Grand Prix ever held, in Amsterdam 1997.

The match-up is Zoo (Dijt) against Reanimator (Müller). Before the match, Müller told me: "This is going to be a lot harder than the deck lists suggest." When Dijt arrived at the table, he jokingly remarked: "This is actually going to be my first play-test match against Reanimator."

Dijt won the roll and had [Wild Nacatl](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wild+Nacatl) on turn one, followed by [Gaddock Teeg](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gaddock+Teeg) on two. Müller responded with [Force of Will](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Force+of+Will) (exiling [Daze](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Daze) ) and had [Entomb](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Entomb) on Dijt's end of turn, binning [Iona, Shield of Emeria](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Iona%2C+Shield+of+Emeria) . His main-phase [Brainstorm](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Brainstorm) , however, yielded no option to reanimate the protective Angel Legend just yet.

Meanwhile Dijt was lacking red mana, but had [Wasteland](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wasteland) to deny Müller his blue. [Loam Lion](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Loam+Lion) also made his way onto the battlefield. The German had [Polluted Delta](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Polluted+Delta) , though, and cast [Careful Study](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Careful+Study) off a basic [Island](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Island) , discarding [Empyrial Archangel](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Empyrial+Archangel) . But again, no reanimation.

Dijt fetched [Taiga](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Taiga) , went in for five and cast [Chain Lightning](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Chain+Lightning) . This left Müller at five life. He cast another [Brainstorm](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Brainstorm) and found [Exhume](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Exhume) . Finally. [Iona, Shield of Emeria](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Iona%2C+Shield+of+Emeria) entered the battlefield and hence forbade the use of all red magic. On the other side, [Exhume](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Exhume) also allowed Dijt to return his previously countered [Gaddock Teeg](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gaddock+Teeg) . His now three creatures attacked, [Wild Nacatl](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wild+Nacatl) on a suicide mission, Teeg and [Loam Lion](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Loam+Lion) on a mission to put Müller at one.

Müller passed the turn without any action, facing two lethal attackers. When these smashed in, a surprise [Echoing Truth](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Echoing+Truth) bounced [Loam Lion](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Loam+Lion) , while infinitely less surprisingly Iona ate [Gaddock Teeg](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gaddock+Teeg) .

On his next turn, Müller completed this remarkable comeback, exhuming [Empyrial Archangel](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Empyrial+Archangel) . The [Archangel](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Archangel) ‘s damage redirection effect allowed him to safely attack with Iona to put Dijt at 11, even though [Exhume](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Exhume) meant yet another return trip for [Gaddock Teeg](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gaddock+Teeg) . Dijt didn't draw an answer and Müller turned his 7/7 and 5/8 sideways.

Sven Dijt 0 – 1 Andreas Müller

![](https://i.ormos.uk/b0/7e/b07e0d7cf0a87633fca16706a0309a03c8078ca6e4e0ebd54f79f49e4bd158c9.jpg)*Andreas Müller*

During sideboarding the players chatted about old times. Well, mostly Dijt. With a little help from Magic historian Richard Hagon they figured out that Müller had been ten years old, when Dijt was last playing in a GP top eight.

Dijt started the second game in quick fashion with [Taiga](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Taiga) , [Kird Ape](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kird+Ape) , [Tormod's Crypt](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tormod%27s+Crypt) , followed by two [Wasteland](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wasteland) s. Stuck on one mana and with the imminent threat of [Tormod's Crypt](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tormod%27s+Crypt) exiling his very own crypt, Müller was in a lot of trouble.

As soon as he got to two mana, however, Müller dropped the bomb. Or rather the bomb, to drop the bomb with. [Show and Tell](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Show+and+Tell) off [Dark Ritual](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dark+Ritual) allowed him to by-pass his graveyard entirely, putting down [Empyrial Archangel](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Empyrial+Archangel) directly from his hand onto the battlefield. Dijt made a face and put down a permanent of his own: [Arid Mesa](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Arid+Mesa) .

Three attacks later the Dutch still had not found an answer to the 5/8 shrouded damage-absorbing flier. Dijt died, Müller advanced to the semifinals.

Sven Dijt 0 – 2 Andreas Müller

## Quarterfinal:Libarary Fine - Lluis Restoy vs. Richard Bland

*by Rich Hagon*

It's a quite staggering achievement to still be playing at this late stage in the largest Magic event ever seen. It also requires, depending on your point of view, either:

(a) A passionate and hopeful imagination, able to visualize yourself dominating a field of over two thousand two hundred players and holding the trophy aloft.

Or

(b) Precisely no imagination whatsoever, ignoring utterly the improbability of winning round after round after round.

As the players shuffled up for their Grand Prix Top 8 debuts, it was unclear which of these applied to them.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/40/43/4043cc60f411feb016520fb55f3a7d0e2e125109d1f79c76f750e71a45a9ded0.jpg)*Lluis Restoy*

Restoy won the die roll and opened up with an uncracked [Windswept Heath](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Windswept+Heath) . [Wooded Foothills](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wooded+Foothills) for Bland had an immediate use, turning into [Taiga](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Taiga) , and powering out [Kird Ape](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kird+Ape) . Restoy's [Windswept Heath](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Windswept+Heath) became [Tropical Island](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tropical+Island) , and he added turn two [Plains](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Plains) before casting [Sylvan Library](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sylvan+Library) , a card Bland was no stranger to.

The [Kird Ape](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kird+Ape) dealt the opening damage of the match, and was joined by [Wild Nacatl](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wild+Nacatl) . [Sylvan Library](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sylvan+Library) sucked extra cards into Restoy's hand, but against Zoo he would have to be careful. He chose not to pay any life, and cast [Kitchen Finks](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kitchen+Finks) to go back to 18 life, a total Bland stood at as he cracked a [Windswept Heath](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Windswept+Heath) . [Path to Exile](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Path+to+Exile) looked to, er, exile the [Kitchen Finks](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kitchen+Finks) , but a discarded [Daze](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Daze) to [Force of Will](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Force+of+Will) scuppered that plan. Bland had a second [Path to Exile](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Path+to+Exile) , and this time Restoy had no counter. In came [Wild Nacatl](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wild+Nacatl) and [Kird Ape](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kird+Ape) , and a second Nacatl completed a fine turn for the Englishman.

Once again, Restoy declined to pay from the [Sylvan Library](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sylvan+Library) . Bland moved to attack, but [Bant Charm](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bant+Charm) sent one Nacatl to the bottom of his library. Out came [Knight of the Reliquary](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Knight+of+the+Reliquary) and another [Wild Nacatl](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wild+Nacatl) , and Restoy had no answer. The power of Zoo had propelled Bland to the opening game.

Lluis Restoy 0 – 1 Richard Bland

Both players began with an opening seven, and [Noble Hierarch](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Noble+Hierarch) was straight out of the gate for Restoy. [Windswept Heath](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Windswept+Heath) into [Taiga](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Taiga) allowed Bland to cast [Grim Lavamancer](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Grim+Lavamancer) . That didn't last long, as Restoy used [Brainstorm](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Brainstorm) before sending the Lavamancer packing with [Swords to Plowshares](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Swords+to+Plowshares) . [Lightning Bolt](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lightning+Bolt) dealt with Restoy's Hierarch, but a second attempt at making [Grim Lavamancer](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Grim+Lavamancer) never got off the ground, with [Daze](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Daze) being more than just [Force of Will](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Force+of+Will) fodder.

Down came [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf) for Restoy, and this was a much, much better start to this must-win game for the Spaniard. Bland offered [Gaddock Teeg](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gaddock+Teeg) , and finally he had some presence on the board, albeit smaller than the 3/4 [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf) . That was bad news for Bland, but so was the second [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf) that followed it onto the battlefield.

[Grim Lavamancer](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Grim+Lavamancer) number three hit play for Bland, who added super-Equipment [Umezawa's Jitte](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Umezawa%27s+Jitte) to the board. Three more damage from one fearless [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf) dropped him to thirteen, and [Sylvan Library](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sylvan+Library) for Restoy looked like being rather more use than in the previous encounter. [Lightning Helix](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lightning+Helix) aimed at the untapped [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf) allowed Bland to finish it off with [Grim Lavamancer](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Grim+Lavamancer) , and finally he was able to deal some damage, putting Restoy to eighteen with [Gaddock Teeg](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gaddock+Teeg) , a card not exactly noted for his beatstick qualities.

[Bant Charm](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bant+Charm) banished Bland's Jitte, and with the [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf) attacking as a 4/5 he was now down to twelve. He attempted the same [Lightning Helix](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lightning+Helix) + [Grim Lavamancer](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Grim+Lavamancer) activation, but Restoy had [Daze](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Daze) ready for the Helix. As Plan B (what else would Bland have?), both [Gaddock Teeg](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gaddock+Teeg) and the Lavamancer attacked. A fetchland allowed Restoy to find [Dryad Arbor](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dryad+Arbor) , which promptly blocked and traded with the [Grim Lavamancer](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Grim+Lavamancer) . If you're sitting and thinking this was a tasty turn for Restoy, I do believe you are correct.

[Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf) smashed for four again, and Restoy added a second. One attacked, trading for one of a pair of blocking [Wild Nacatl](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wild+Nacatl) s, but the threats kept on coming, this time in the form of [Rhox War Monk](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rhox+War+Monk) . Bland needed to stablize the board, and he did so with a [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf) of his own. While the Englishman was relying on the top of his deck, Restoy's library was of the Sylvan variety. That allowed him to massively improve his draw quality, and Bland's [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf) bit the dust courtesy of [Swords to Plowshares](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Swords+to+Plowshares) .

The fantastic trading blows kept on coming. [Path to Exile](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Path+to+Exile) from Bland dealt with [Rhox War Monk](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rhox+War+Monk) . Restoy finally paid four life for the privilege of [Sylvan Library](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sylvan+Library) bonus cardage, but Bland now had momentum, with [Gaddock Teeg](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gaddock+Teeg) and the last remaining [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf) . [Kitchen Finks](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kitchen+Finks) for Restoy got in the way, and just like Restoy, was most persistant. Bland kept on coming, with [Kird Ape](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kird+Ape) the next onto an increasingly-English-tilting battlefield.

[Brainstorm](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Brainstorm) from Restoy.... [Brainstorm](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Brainstorm) from Restoy....dig, dig, dig.... He finally tapped out for another [Kitchen Finks](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kitchen+Finks) , and was back up to thirteen. Even so, the 4/5 [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf) was the big player on the board. [Brainstorm](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Brainstorm) again from Restoy, at the cost of a Sylvan payment, and he was back to nine. [Qasali Pridemage](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Qasali+Pridemage) looked to add some cover for the Spaniard. Since irony is practically a national sport in England, it was probably not lost on Bland that it was one of his own best-performing cards – [Sylvan Library](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sylvan+Library) – that was currently so effectively blocking his route to the semifinals.

When [Kitchen Finks](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kitchen+Finks) attacked, Bland smelled a rat, but couldn't tell quite where the smell was coming from. He blocked with [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf) , and then saw Restoy cast and crack [Relic of Progenitus](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Relic+of+Progenitus) , thereby killing the [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf) . Now the pendulum was right back with Restoy in a bare-knuckle carry-him-out-on-his-shield Game 2. Could Bland match the combat shenanigans of his opponent? He attacked with [Kird Ape](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kird+Ape) , faced a triple block, and traded for both [Kitchen Finks](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kitchen+Finks) , finally sending the lifegainers from the board.

[Noble Hierarch](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Noble+Hierarch) for Restoy meant his [Qasali Pridemage](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Qasali+Pridemage) could attack as a 4/4, and he paid four life in order to get [Kitchen Finks](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kitchen+Finks) down a turn ahead of schedule. Bland was down to six, with Restoy at eight, and still anything could happen. [Wild Nacatl](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wild+Nacatl) ....go. Well, this isn't Hollywood. And Bland knew he wasn't writing the script when Restoy added [Kitchen Finks](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kitchen+Finks) number four. Even Legacy Zoo decks aren't designed to deal thirty-some damage, and Bland elected to move to a decider, knowing that at least he'd be on the play.

Lluis Restoy 1 – 1 Richard Bland

![](https://i.ormos.uk/78/cd/78cd0ed32620a478410e45d804581bfbf187c6ece13441dac9f331dbd444afee.jpg)*Richard Bland*

The "I'm on the play" edge for Bland increased when Restoy mulliganed to six. If the self-inflicted bruise was anything to go by, Restoy wouldn't be keeping his six either. It took him a while, but in the end the disciplined decision won. Five it would be.

[Arid Mesa](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Arid+Mesa) got Bland cracking, and out came [Wild Nacatl](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wild+Nacatl) . [Tropical Island](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tropical+Island) opened for Restoy, and Bland added [Mountain](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mountain) before beating for three. Second Nacatl resolved, but a potentially backbreaking [Kird Ape](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kird+Ape) did not, defeated by a defensive [Daze](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Daze) . Land for Restoy, and again he passed. Six more damage crashed across. Bland added [Grim Lavamancer](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Grim+Lavamancer) , and it looked all but over.

Still nothing from Restoy, and now he was at just four life. A second [Grim Lavamancer](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Grim+Lavamancer) seemed academic, and three mana couldn't save Restoy. In an anti-climactic Game 3, Zoo did what Zoo does best – defy opponents to stumble.

Lluis Restoy 1 –2 Richard Bland

## Quarterfinal - Tomoharu Saito vs. Ruben Gonzales Parrado

*Luis Scott-Vargas*

In his Top 8 questionnaire, Saito claimed that Countertop Goyf was the only bad matchup for his [Ad Nauseam](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ad+Nauseam) deck, and that is indeed the matchup he now faces. The theoretically good matchup may be little consolation for Ruben Gonzales Parrado, as he faces the undoubtedly most experienced player in the Top 8 of this enormous Grand Prix.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/c5/d4/c5d416df1d3663c921cb855218bdd4628e21a4c4e5d507ce6af3b4316de2ad57.jpg)*Tomoharu Saito*

### Game 1

Saito wins the ever important die roll, and to top it off, Parrado mulligans to six. He grimaces and keeps his hand, which prompts Saito to consider his first play. After a few moments of thought, Saito fires away, with an [Underground Sea](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Underground+Sea) and a mainphase [Mystical Tutor](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mystical+Tutor) for the namesake of his deck, [Ad Nauseam](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ad+Nauseam) .

Parrado's next turn is less impressive, as he simply plays a [Tropical Island](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tropical+Island) and passes. Saito, having drawn the [Ad Nauseam](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ad+Nauseam) , again goes back in the tank to determine his options. Playing against a deck that sports [Force of Will](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Force+of+Will) , [Daze](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Daze) , and a fast clock certainly puts him under pressure, and he contents himself with playing a land and a [Chrome Mox](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Chrome+Mox) , imprinting [Infernal Tutor](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Infernal+Tutor) .

Again, Parrado has a fast turn, as he develops his board with a [Volcanic Island](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Volcanic+Island) and a [Noble Hierarch](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Noble+Hierarch) . At the end of his turn, Saito fetches for an [Underground Sea](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Underground+Sea) but chooses not to cast anything.

On Saito's turn, the action starts. He casts [Cabal Ritual](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cabal+Ritual) (without Threshold), then plays [City of Traitors](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=City+of+Traitors) and [Ad Nauseam](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ad+Nauseam) . After a [Brainstorm](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Brainstorm) , Parrado hesitantly lets the [Ad Nauseam](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ad+Nauseam) resolve with a soft "Ok". This excites both the crowd and Saito, who confirms "OK!" quite energetically.

With Saito at 19, [Ad Nauseam](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ad+Nauseam) is more than likely lethal. He flips, in order:

[Dark Ritual](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dark+Ritual)  
[Ad Nauseam](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ad+Nauseam) (prompting more than a few "oohs" from the crowd)  
[Lion's Eye Diamond](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lion%27s+Eye+Diamond)  
[Mystical Tutor](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mystical+Tutor)  
[Tendrils of Agony](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tendrils+of+Agony)  
[Sensei's Divining Top](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sensei%27s+Divining+Top)  
[Cabal Ritual](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cabal+Ritual)  
[Polluted Delta](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Polluted+Delta)  
[Chrome Mox](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Chrome+Mox)  
[Underground Sea](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Underground+Sea)  
[Brainstorm](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Brainstorm)

After some counting, Saito decides that is sufficient, and puts those 11 cards into his hand. He imprints [Ad Nauseam](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ad+Nauseam) on [Chrome Mox](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Chrome+Mox) , then casts [Dark Ritual](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dark+Ritual) and Lion's Eye Diamond. [Brainstorm](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Brainstorm) follows, then [Cabal Ritual](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cabal+Ritual) , then a [Tendrils of Agony](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tendrils+of+Agony) for 18. Not quite lethal, but the 36-point life swing will presumably buy Saito enough time to find another copy of Tendrils.

Parrado [Ponder](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ponder) s on his turn, then plays a [Flooded Strand](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Flooded+Strand) and attacks for one with [Noble Hierarch](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Noble+Hierarch) , which draws some chuckles from the ever-growing crowd.

On his turn, Saito [Mystical Tutor](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mystical+Tutor) s during his upkeep, prompting a concession when he reveals that he will indeed search for [Tendrils of Agony](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tendrils+of+Agony) .

Tomoharu Saito 1 – 0 Ruben Gonzales Parrado

![](https://i.ormos.uk/9a/ad/9aad2bb24f433beeb3f88bfbaf5d6bd7e8e7677b339ec87846c898df1ce325bd.jpg)*Ruben Gonzales Parrado*

### Game 2

Parrado begins the game with another mulligan, with Saito rapidly deciding to keep. Parrado keeps his six, and again only has a [Tropical Island](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tropical+Island) on his first turn. Saito's [Flooded Strand](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Flooded+Strand) finds him an [Underground Sea](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Underground+Sea) and a broken sleeve, which he quickly replaces. [Lotus Petal](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lotus+Petal) follows, [Daze](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Daze) protection for the [Thoughtseize](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thoughtseize) he plays next. Parrado responds with a [Brainstorm](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Brainstorm) , likely planning on hiding his best cards.

He fans out his hand, revealing [Ponder](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ponder) , [Swords to Plowshares](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Swords+to+Plowshares) , [Rhox War Monk](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rhox+War+Monk) , [Plains](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Plains) , and [Misty Rainforest](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Misty+Rainforest) , and Saito quickly takes the [Ponder](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ponder) .

The hidden cards are yet to be seen, as Parrado only plays [Misty Rainforest](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Misty+Rainforest) on his next turn. Saito doesn't do much more, only [Ponder](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ponder) ing and playing a land before passing.

Parrado decides that the time isn't ripe for him to cast any spells mainphase, and just contents himself with playing a third land. Saito also has a third land, and he too passes the turn after some counting.

[Rhox War Monk](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rhox+War+Monk) for Parrado breaks the stalemate, which Saito follows with an end of turn [Mystical Tutor](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mystical+Tutor) . The Tutor fetches a [Duress](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Duress) , signalling a pretty strong hand for the Japanese player. [Duress](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Duress) takes [Dispel](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dispel) , leaving Parrado with only a [Swords to Plowshares](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Swords+to+Plowshares) and an [Island](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Island) . If Tomoharu has what it takes to go off, Parrado will be unable to disrupt him.

[Cabal Ritual](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cabal+Ritual) and [Ad Nauseam](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ad+Nauseam) are Saito's next two plays, which gives Saito 15 life, a [Lotus Petal](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lotus+Petal) , and a land drop to play with.

[Ad Nauseam](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ad+Nauseam) reveals:

[Cabal Ritual](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cabal+Ritual)  
[Mystical Tutor](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mystical+Tutor)  
[Infernal Tutor](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Infernal+Tutor)  
[Mystical Tutor](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mystical+Tutor)  
[City of Traitors](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=City+of+Traitors)

At this point, Saito pauses to do some calculation, but ultimately decides to continue.

After revealing just [Thoughtseize](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thoughtseize) , Saito picks up the pile of cards. In a flash, he plays Mox, [Mystical Tutor](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mystical+Tutor) , [Dark Ritual](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dark+Ritual) , [Cabal Ritual](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cabal+Ritual) , [Thoughtseize](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thoughtseize) , and a now-hellbent [Infernal Tutor](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Infernal+Tutor) , which finds the tenth spell of the turn, [Tendrils of Agony](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tendrils+of+Agony) .

Tomoharu Saito 2 – 0 Ruben Gonzales Parrado

## Semifinal - Tomoharu Saito vs David Do Anh

*by Tim Willoughby*

![](https://i.ormos.uk/f7/8c/f78ca8e252b443520e2cd017af82def096e914e9cfc1db6ffa262c0dedcbd4e6.jpg)*David Do Anh*

For the semifinals of Grand Prix Madrid, we have an [Ad Nauseam](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ad+Nauseam) Tendrils mirror. While the decks are very similar, the players are quite different. Tomoharu Saito has a Player of the Year title and huge Pro Tour success to his name, the same of which cannot yet be said of 16 year old Czech player David Do Anh.

What David does have going for him though is his record with the deck he's playing today. He has played a total of 3 Legacy tournaments. All have been with this deck. He has won all of them. [Ad Nauseam](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ad+Nauseam) is what he knows, and he romped to 2nd place in the swiss with the deck, so he is well qualified to be here.

Saito won the roll, but had a mulligan on the play, finally leading with [Underground Sea](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Underground+Sea) and a Sensei's Divining Top. Do Anh had [Flooded Strand](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Flooded+Strand) and nothing further to begin with.

Saito began doing math from the second turn, working out what he needed to do to end the game fast. After a little thought, he looked at the top 3 cards of his deck with Sensei's Divining Top in his upkeep. He didn't have much more for the turn though, with just a [Flooded Strand](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Flooded+Strand) . David cracked his Strand at the end of turn, and went for a [Tundra](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tundra) . Access to white mana would prove important, as Orim's Chant in Do Anh's deck would allow him to disrupt any attempt from Saito to go off. Saito had no such luxury, and would have to use discard spells to clear the way.

David played an [Underground Sea](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Underground+Sea) , and used it to cast his own Sensei's Divining Top. This would be a game of carefully building position, before brief flurries of activity. Winning with Storm rather requires such activity.

The first big play of the game came from Saito, who played a [Mystical Tutor](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mystical+Tutor) to fetch [Ad Nauseam](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ad+Nauseam) , then used Sensei's Divining Top to draw it. A [Dark Ritual](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dark+Ritual) from Saito was responded to by Do Anh with Orim's Chant. There would be no big storm for Saito this turn.

The first barrage from Saito had been weathered by David Do Anh, and now it was his turn to build up to something. He used Sensei's Divining Top, then a fetchland, followed by [Brainstorm](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Brainstorm) to give him a great many options. A [Mystical Tutor](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mystical+Tutor) then let him both shuffle away those cards he didn't want, and find a [Cabal Ritual](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cabal+Ritual) , which would be worth a lot of mana given that Do Anh now had threshold.

Two [Cabal Ritual](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cabal+Ritual) s from Do Anh made 8 mana. Then came [Ad Nauseam](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ad+Nauseam) . It revealed

[Dark Ritual](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dark+Ritual)  
[Ill-Gotten Gains](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ill-Gotten+Gains)  
[Dark Ritual](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dark+Ritual)  
[Orim's Chant](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Orim%27s+Chant)  
[Dark Ritual](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dark+Ritual)  
[Wipe Away](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wipe+Away)  
[Mystical Tutor](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mystical+Tutor)  
[Duress](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Duress)

These put Do Anh on four life, as he had already used some fetchlands.

"I have a chance!" declared Saito, who looked on as Do Anh stopped with the [Ad Nauseam](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ad+Nauseam) , and started casting [Dark Ritual](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dark+Ritual) s.

The 16 year old Czech player built up both storm and mana, casting [Duress](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Duress) to snag that [Ad Nauseam](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ad+Nauseam) of Saito's (seeing an [Infernal Tutor](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Infernal+Tutor) and [Lotus Petal](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lotus+Petal) as well). [Mystical Tutor](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mystical+Tutor) from Do Anh found [Tendrils of Agony](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tendrils+of+Agony) , and Sensei's Divining Top drew it.

[Infernal Tutor](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Infernal+Tutor) found another Orim's Chant and upped the storm count to make [Tendrils of Agony](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tendrils+of+Agony) lethal.

Tomoharu Saito 0 – 1 David Do Anh

![](https://i.ormos.uk/f4/75/f475f72e1e2f8f76d6c37cddaa89afc0a1bd392e19c41bd9a19356ed1f15d8d9.jpg)*Tomoharu Saito*

Post sideboard games promised to be interesting, as both players had [Sadistic Sacrament](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sadistic+Sacrament) available to them, which could happily remove the other's win conditions. There was a real chance that [Dark Confidant](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dark+Confidant) beatdown could decide the match.

Saito kept on the play, dropping a [Misty Rainforest](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Misty+Rainforest) onto the table and passing. Do Anh had a [Tropical Island](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tropical+Island) into [Ponder](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ponder) . Saito fetched an [Underground Sea](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Underground+Sea) at the end of turn, and untapped into a turn two [Dark Confidant](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dark+Confidant) . The beatdown plan was on.

Do Anh fetched an [Underground Sea](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Underground+Sea) for his second turn, and played a [Chrome Mox](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Chrome+Mox) , imprinting [Mystical Tutor](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mystical+Tutor) . Next came Lion's Eye Diamond. Saito started to laugh, he might just be hit by the combo after all. [Infernal Tutor](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Infernal+Tutor) was responded to by Do Anh by cracking his [Lion's Eye Diamond](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lion%27s+Eye+Diamond) . Do Anh had 4 black mana, and fetched [Ad Nauseam](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ad+Nauseam) . He tapped his [Tropical Island](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tropical+Island) to help cast it, and

[Cabal Ritual](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cabal+Ritual)  
[Brainstorm](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Brainstorm)  
[Mystical Tutor](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mystical+Tutor)  
[Extirpate](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Extirpate)  
[Lotus Petal](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lotus+Petal)  
[Lion's Eye Diamond](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lion%27s+Eye+Diamond)  
[Sensei's Divining Top](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sensei%27s+Divining+Top)  
[Tundra](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tundra)  
[Dark Ritual](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dark+Ritual)  
[Dark Ritual](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dark+Ritual)  
[Ill-Gotten Gains](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ill-Gotten+Gains)

Still more cards came, and Do Anh went down to just one life before stopping. He stormed up and made a hefty amount of mana before using [Ill-Gotten Gains](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ill-Gotten+Gains) to get back some cards, most importantly [Tendrils of Agony](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tendrils+of+Agony) . Saito had no disruption, and was soon on the wrong end of a very much lethal Tendrils.

David Do Anh wins 2 – 0, advancing to the finals.

## Semifinal:An A-List Animated Affair - Andreas Müller vs. Richard Bland

*by Rich Hagon*

All day yesterday, and all day today, Richard Bland has punished decks for not quite performing at optimum level. That's what Zoo does – simply devours mediocrity, error, stumble, or fumble. Across the table, Andreas Müller has the explosive Reanimator strategy, and that can put Zoo in the bin faster than you can say “in the bin”. Even if you can say it really fast.

Both players kept, with Müller on the play opening on “go”. That's right, the first turn of the semifinal saw no land for the German, but with Reanimator decks, that might be a blessing. After Bland cast [Wild Nacatl](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wild+Nacatl) , Müller was able to spend his second turn discarding [Sphinx of the Steel Wind](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sphinx+of+the+Steel+Wind) . In came the [Wild Nacatl](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wild+Nacatl) , with [Kird Ape](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kird+Ape) and then [Chain Lightning](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Chain+Lightning) completing the turn, Müller now at fourteen.

At last, the first land arrived on the German side, a [Polluted Delta](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Polluted+Delta) that became [Underground Sea](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Underground+Sea) . [Reanimate](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Reanimate) brought the Sphinx onto the battlefield, but the German was down to just five life. Could Bland apply the burn to take Game 1? [Chain Lightning](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Chain+Lightning) ....Müller at two.... [Lightning Bolt](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lightning+Bolt) ....

[Force of Will](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Force+of+Will) ! Müller at one! Literally spine-tingling Magic. Did Bland have one more burn spell?

“Go.”

The sweetest two letters ever heard for Müller. In came the Sphinx, and Müller was back to seven, and it was hard to see Bland winning from here. [Brainstorm](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Brainstorm) at end of turn for Müller only added to the fact that there was a 6/6 flying, vigilance, first strike, lifelink, protection from red and from green monster on the table. Bland was down to seven, Müller up to twelve, and it was all one-way traffic. Bland swept up his permanents, having ended a lightning-fast Game 1 just one lightning point short.

Andreas Müller 1, Richard Bland 0.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/1c/d8/1cd81083b6423ceb94d0fccfb85e118f646dcfc3e107e3b2d8c7ca87b00befb7.jpg)*Andreas Muller*

As soon as he uttered the words “So close, so close”, it was clear that Bland was heading for six cards. [Kird Ape](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kird+Ape) and [Tormod's Crypt](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tormod%27s+Crypt) opened the game, and Crypt isn't shabby against a deck that relies on the graveyard. Müller opened on [Careful Study](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Careful+Study) , sending a land and [Iona, Shield of Emeria](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Iona%2C+Shield+of+Emeria) to the bin. In came a thoroughly unimpressive [Kird Ape](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kird+Ape) as a 1/1, and [Chain Lightning](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Chain+Lightning) sent Müller to sixteen, rather than the fifteen he was expecting. “Strong”, deadpanned Bland.

[Reanimate](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Reanimate) for Müller left the Englishman no choice but to blow his [Tormod's Crypt](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tormod%27s+Crypt) , emptying the German graveyard. Another 1/1 Ape attack, another [Chain Lightning](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Chain+Lightning) , and Müller was at twelve, in a weird version of beatdown. [Grim Lavamancer](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Grim+Lavamancer) joined the timid little 1/1, leaving Bland just two cards in hand after his mulligan.

With more pain coming from fetchlands, Müller slipped into single digits, and cast [Show and Tell](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Show+and+Tell) . It resolved, and while Bland got [Sylvan Library](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sylvan+Library) , Müller had [Empyrial Archangel](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Empyrial+Archangel) . Sure, it flies. Sure, it has Shroud. Sure, it has five power. More to the point, it has EIGHT toughness, and everything goes her way. She is, in common parlance, a kicking.

Bland wasn't going to give up easily. He paid four life to the [Sylvan Library](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sylvan+Library) , and passed with three cards in hand. In came the [Archangel](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Archangel) , dropping the Englishman to eleven. Müller passed, seemingly in control. So often a gamebreaker for Bland, could [Sylvan Library](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sylvan+Library) come to his rescue at the last moment? He could drop to just three and take all the cards available, be picky and drop to seven, or remain at eleven with no significant Sylvan edge. That was the path he chose, attacking with just the [Kird Ape](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kird+Ape) , and falling to six when the [Empyrial Archangel](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Empyrial+Archangel) smashed again. Müller stood at nine, but unless the [Archangel](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Archangel) could be defeated, that nine might as well be infinite. Bland, with no better use for it, cast [Path to Exile](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Path+to+Exile) on his own [Kird Ape](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kird+Ape) , searching out a land and at least allowing him one more possible look into his [Sylvan Library](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sylvan+Library) future, a future that looked likely to have death written all over it.

[Chain Lightning](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Chain+Lightning) – resolve.  
[Lightning Bolt](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lightning+Bolt) – resolve.  
[Grim Lavamancer](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Grim+Lavamancer) activation – resolve.

The [Empyrial Archangel](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Empyrial+Archangel) was dead. Dead dead dead dead dead, deader than a dead thing. D E A D spells Dead.

Müller cast [Exhume](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Exhume) .

“ [Chain Lightning](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Chain+Lightning) you.  
[Lightning Helix](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lightning+Helix) you.  
[Grim Lavamancer](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Grim+Lavamancer) activation, you.”

The [Empyrial Archangel](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Empyrial+Archangel) was dead. No, not a misprint, actually in the bin, not breathing, dead.

Müller cast [Exhume](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Exhume) . Bland cast [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf) . These things are not the same...

Müller went right back to work, with [Mystical Tutor](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mystical+Tutor) searching up [Misdirection](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Misdirection) , to the accompanying sound of a door being thoroughly nailed shut. Bland wasn't dead, but he was on the kind of life support that only the mad or the British would try to survive. He blew up his own [Qasali Pridemage](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Qasali+Pridemage) to put [Sylvan Library](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sylvan+Library) in the bin, making [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf) truly enormous, and then activating the [Grim Lavamancer](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Grim+Lavamancer) to finish the [Empyrial Archangel](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Empyrial+Archangel) for the third time.

Ding dong, the witch is dead etc, etc, etc.

The crowd went NUTS. Twenty-four points of damage had been dealt to the Angel, and now, against all possible reason, Müller was into the tank, with a grip of doom against nothing in Bland's hand, but facing down a giant [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf) and a small [Grim Lavamancer](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Grim+Lavamancer) . His choice was [Perish](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Perish) , and Bland laid a [Wooded Foothills](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wooded+Foothills) before leaving [Grim Lavamancer](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Grim+Lavamancer) back, ready to start eating his own graveyard in pursuit of victory.

[Careful Study](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Careful+Study) from Müller, discarding two [Reanimate](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Reanimate) , cards he wouldn't be casting any time soon. Bland fetched yet another dual land and dropped to two, before activating his Lavamancer, sending Müller to seven. Implausible. Utterly, utterly implausible.

Bland added a second Lavamancer, leaving Müller to cast [Entomb](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Entomb) . [Sphinx of the Steel Wind](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sphinx+of+the+Steel+Wind) hit the graveyard, practically begging to join the fray somehow. Müller passed, and another two cards got eaten, dropping Müller to five. Bland found [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf) on top of his deck, and when he cast it, Müller responded with [Mystical Tutor](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mystical+Tutor) , searching up [Exhume](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Exhume) . The [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf) resolved, and Bland went deep into thought, desperately trying to avoid a fourth encounter with the mighty Angel. Both Lavamancers attacked, leaving Müller at three.

The German drew his [Exhume](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Exhume) , but now led by the narrowest of margins at three life to two. A crowd six-deep had gathered to witness this rearguard action by the Brit Bland. The German [Exhume](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Exhume) returned [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf) for Bland, and [Sphinx of the Steel Wind](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sphinx+of+the+Steel+Wind) for Müller. He passed, and the crowd held its collective breath.

In came all three monsters. The Sphinx gained six life for Müller, but the second [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf) dealt six right back, leaving him at three. [Grim Lavamancer](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Grim+Lavamancer) dealt one, and the second Lavamancer was able to swallow the last two cards from Bland's graveyard, completing one of the most miraculous comebacks in Magic history.

Andreas Müller 1 Richard Bland 1.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/ba/e3/bae37f006de789fdc0b89c53ed96f04d00bc32d4be6efd7dfd58c391e17db904.jpg)*Richard Bland*

Bonkers.

For Game 3, Müller liked his opening seven, presumably still wondering exactly what he was doing still playing in the semis, and not waiting for the final to start. Bland, meanwhile, sent seven away, aware that despite those second-game heroics, he was facing an amazingly explosive deck, on the draw. Land opened for Müller, with the huge crowd still buzzing. Bland began with [Tormod's Crypt](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tormod%27s+Crypt) , a card that might at least buy him a little time. When he attempted to add [Wild Nacatl](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wild+Nacatl) , Müller responded with [Mystical Tutor](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mystical+Tutor) , finding [Brainstorm](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Brainstorm) , before using [Daze](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Daze) to counter the Nacatl. Bland tried again, with [Qasali Pridemage](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Qasali+Pridemage) causing Müller to respond with [Brainstorm](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Brainstorm) . Another counter? Or just the right time to cast the Instant? [Daze](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Daze) again, and the board was empty of creatures for Bland, and empty of everything for Müller.

A second [Mystical Tutor](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mystical+Tutor) for Müller brought him [Show and Tell](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Show+and+Tell) , not a card particularly good for Bland's heart rate. He laid land number two, and passed, but the lack of action from Bland was significant. [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf) met yet another counterspell, and this time it was [Force of Will](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Force+of+Will) , removing [Daze](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Daze) . Land number three for Müller, but presumably nothing to power out with [Show and Tell](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Show+and+Tell) . Bland passed yet again, and the hushed expectation among the huge crowd continued to grow.

[Careful Study](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Careful+Study) drew Müller two cards deeper, discarding two land, and passing. With the long-ago cast [Tormod's Crypt](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tormod%27s+Crypt) in play, the German was looking to bypass the graveyard route entirely with his blue Urza's Saga Sorcery. [Brainstorm](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Brainstorm) , and you could almost hear the sound of him digging through the library. What on earth did Bland have in hand that he would pass so many turns running? Nothing but land would be the most obvious and most disappointing answer, but he had shown throughout the weekend an inability to lay down and die.

Three land for Müller. Three words for Müller. Show. And. Tell.

Müller had [Sphinx of the Steel Wind](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sphinx+of+the+Steel+Wind) . Bland put a [Mountain](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mountain) into play. These two are not the same either...

Bland cast [Grim Lavamancer](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Grim+Lavamancer) , but that “nothing but land” looked increasingly likely. Everything he'd thrown at Müller had been countered in the early game, and now Müller had established a mighty bridgehead to the final, leading by twenty-three to ten. [Reanimate](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Reanimate) wanted to bring Bland's [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf) back to life, and losing two life barely put a dent in the German life total, at twenty-one.

[Wild Nacatl](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wild+Nacatl) surely wasn't relevant. Bland fell to four, destroying what was ultimately his own [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf) , and now needed [Path to Exile](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Path+to+Exile) very, very badly. He cast [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf) , and passed the turn. In Müller's upkeep, Bland forced himself to keep breathing via [Lightning Helix](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lightning+Helix) , but trailed thirty life to one.

All good things, except possibly pizza, must come to an end, and there was no fairytale [Path to Exile](https://web.archive.org/web/20211128115823/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Path+to+Exile) waiting for Bland to prolong the agony any longer. Match 7,756 of 7,757 at the biggest Magic event of all time might very well have been the very, very best.

Andre Müller 2 Richard Bland 1.

*by Rich Hagon*

*   [**The Final**](https://media.ormos.uk/41/e1/41e12acd12296e8729c0956531160cae9e9b109dd06ff3f3848fd3fafaf00da4.mp3) *by Magic* (2.3MB)
*   [**Jace Goes Penultimate**](https://media.ormos.uk/88/6e/886e9fe2b0117489777578145c43d5fff3a4287b63c10bbe54c91c91a1a43124.mp3) *by Magic* (15.7MB)
